# Attribution, chronology and status

This record is part of the scientific evidence, not a substitute for running or understanding the code.

| Material | Origin / attribution | Status in this release |
|---|---|---|
| SCADA, nominal model, configuration | Vrachimis et al., original Zenodo record 4017659, CC-BY-4.0 | External data; exact bytes checked against official checksums |
| Existing models and published performance | Authors cited in the Milestone 2 bibliography / literature index | Literature-reported; their training was not reproduced |
| n1(+15), local signals and planned comparisons | Project proposal developed through discussion with the student and AI assistance | Proposed research; no claim of algorithmic invention or worldwide priority |
| Descriptive calculations and figures | Project-specific code generated/refined and executed with OpenAI Codex assistance on the project computer | Computationally executed; not represented as independently coded by the student |
| Historical literature collection/grouping | AI-assisted searching, reading and project-specific classification | Curated, incomplete inventory; automatic recount is reproducible |
| Relevance screening and closer examination of six studies | Student reports abstract/targeted-section triage and closer reading in the supplied V3 account | Personal account recorded after feedback; final confirmation pending; not evidence of full code audits or reproduced scores |
| Portable reproduction package | Refactored after professor feedback for this public repository | New public packaging; not claimed to have been accessible when the report was submitted |
| Student's independent execution, understanding or sign-off | Requires an actual student action and an explicit record | Not established by an automated run; no signature or review is invented |

## What has actually been executed

The pipeline checks eight annual SCADA tables and recomputes Jan-Sep 2018 descriptive
statistics, persistence errors, correlations, daily profiles, nominal pipe paths and historical
literature counts. Each completed run records versions and SHA-256 hashes in
`results/reproduced/run_manifest.json`. That manifest supports a particular execution,
not a claim that a student independently designed or wrote every component.

## What remains proposed

Training Gradient Boosting, Ridge or Random Forest; assessing future-pressure prediction
on 2019; determining the incremental value of extra signals; and claiming an improvement
over persistence. None of these outcomes is implied by reproducing the EDA.

## Historical versus newly refactored code

`provenance/historical_scripts/*.py.txt` are unchanged snapshots from the earlier local
project. They retain historical paths, dependencies and report-editing side effects, so they
are **reading material only**. The historical figure bytes and their hashes are also archived.
Use `reproduce.py` and the `repro/` modules for an actual rerun.

The current script refactoring and new public links are post-feedback corrections. They must
not be described retroactively as evidence that was provided in the original submission.

## Plots and quantitative originality

The daily overlays and correlation cells are computed from the released measurements.
The requested daily-overlay presentation was inspired by an illustrative plot supplied during
the project discussion; no other paper's pressure curves or correlation values were copied.
Figure 1 is an interpretive supply schematic, not a new hydraulic simulation. Its original
drawing source and nominal-model inputs are identified in the evidence ledger.

Numerical calculations made from public data are not, by themselves, a new learning method.
Research novelty, correct numerical reproduction and authorship are separate questions.

## Engineering-rationale audit added 2026-10-28

The new [prompt audit](PROMPT_VERIFICATION_AUDIT.md), feedback map, engineering discussion,
Ahmed comparison and proposed experiment plan were prepared with Codex assistance. They
are **draft interpretations pending student review**, not invented personal reflections.
The supplied JSON contains attributed rationale, but does not prove that the student
independently executed the nominal/generator checks. The [review log](STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md)
therefore leaves personal answers and sign-off blank.

A new static geometry procedure checks official nominal and generator files and records
their checksums, code and outputs in [its own manifest](../results/engineering/audit_manifest.json).
It confirms selected elevations but reports differing pipe distances and tank diameter;
it performs no hydraulic simulation or learned-model training. Earlier manifests describe
their own executions and are not retroactively expanded to cover this new module.

Full-text inspection of the prior ASCE article confirms daily overlays and Average curves
in Figure 4, printed p. 05026021-5. The new user-supplied account connects the earlier
illustrative plot to that research. This supports the shared visual convention, while the
personal inspiration/history remains for student confirmation. See [method transfer](PRIOR_RESEARCH_AND_METHOD_TRANSFER.md).
The copyrighted article/figure is not newly redistributed; the new record contains metadata,
locators and a local full-text hash. Current BattLeDIM curves remain newly computed from
external data, with AI assistance disclosed.

The professor response and group pitch are drafts only. No human approval, group membership,
new regression score, field benefit or blind/untouched 2019 test is asserted.

## V3 literature-role clarification added 2026-10-28

The supplied account reports a human contribution to relevance judgments and focused
interpretation of ChebNet, SAM-kNN, AGN, LILA, GATRes and EILF. That role is credited as
**student-reported**, separately from AI-assisted discovery and computable grouping. The

reading dates or execution of authors' code.

with Codex assistance using primary papers and selected pinned code passages. They
establish support for specific reported methods/results, not experimental reproduction.
Draft implications still require the student's review. A formal review/sign-off has not
been supplied; the proposed first-person replacement paragraph is not publicly republished.


# Anticipated research contribution

Post-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. **AI-assisted draft pending student review.** This statement proposes future work; it does not assert independent student implementation or already-demonstrated novelty.

The proposed contribution is a controlled assessment of **whether hydraulically selected measurements add useful information for forecasting recorded n1 pressure 15 minutes ahead, beyond n1 history and calendar information**. The project would compare increasingly rich input sets using the same regression methods, target timestamps, chronological periods and error measures. It would report both improvements and failures to improve, rather than claim a new algorithm.

The V3 account also reports the student's relevance screening and closer examination of
six selected studies. That human interpretive role is distinct from AI-assisted discovery,
automated counts and programming. The [review-method account](../literature/REVIEW_METHOD_AND_STUDENT_ROLE.md)
credits it as student-reported; the [six-study matrix](../literature/SIX_STUDY_CRITICAL_MATRIX.md)
offers a source-checked draft synthesis for confirmation. Neither records independent
execution of the authors' models or proves personal understanding through a CSV entry.

The supplied engineering rationale favors a small tank-fed area with three monitored pressure nodes, measured tank level, pump inflow and a local demand measurement. [Static verification](SECTOR_AND_NODE_SELECTION_RATIONALE.md) supports that scope and n1's relative elevation/remoteness **among monitored nodes**. It does not establish a uniquely critical node or guaranteed feature value. The supplied prior-research context is documented in [method transfer](PRIOR_RESEARCH_AND_METHOD_TRANSFER.md); it is background, not evidence of current forecast performance.

## What the experiments could add

1. **A reproducible preparation protocol:** six signal series, past-only lags and known calendar values, explicit +15-minute labels and common eligible rows. Input availability is a timestamp-based assumption; real telemetry delays are not documented.
2. **An incremental-value result:** distinguish the information in peer pressures, storage/pump signals and local AMR. The central question is not which variable has the highest marginal correlation, but which addition reduces future error after n1 history is already available.
3. **An honest account of effect size:** compare MAE/RMSE in metres, centimetres and relative changes against the same-period persistence baseline. If gains are small, unstable or absent, report that outcome and the limits of the hydraulic selection argument.

[Ahmed et al.](AHMED_2025_COMPARISON.md) already combine the five main C measurements for reconstruction-based leak detection. Our proposed future-pressure target and added AMR comparison are **extensions in task and evaluation**, not proof of worldwide first use. The historical literature inventory is incomplete and cannot establish priority.

## What could contradict the rationale

Persistence already has small descriptive error. Peer pressure signals may be redundant; T1 may describe state already visible in n1; pump flow may be useful only near operating changes or may add nothing; AMR_n1 may add noise. A high-elevation target may not be the most difficult or operationally important one. These are counterarguments the [matched experiments](EXPERIMENTAL_CONTRIBUTION_PLAN.md) must allow to win.

Expected benefit is therefore conditional: a reproducible empirical answer about information value on this simulated network. No causal mechanism, field deployment benefit, error reduction, novelty-by-feature-combination or independent human authorship is claimed. Approval and personal reasoning must be supplied through the [student review log](STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md).

# Prior field research and what can transfer

Post-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. AI-assisted source review and draft context; personal motivation remains pending student confirmation.

Arango-Martinez, A., Rosero-García, E., and Franco-Mejia, E. (2026). *Dynamic Pressure Regulation in Water Distribution Systems through Remote Critical Node Control: A Case Study.* Journal of Water Resources Planning and Management, 152(8), 05026021. [Publisher / DOI](https://doi.org/10.1061/JWRMD5.WRENG-7321). Metadata and the local full-text hash are recorded in [the source-review record](../provenance/prior_asce_source_review.json). Publisher metadata confirms the authorship; it does not establish which current repository actions the student independently performed.

The article uses real-sector pressure/inflow measurements and PRV step tests for low-order dynamic identification and controller design. Its controller assessment is in silico using real operational data; it is **not a completed closed-loop field deployment**. The abstract and the full article's modeling/validation sections distinguish these stages. This is domain background, not another BattLeDIM forecasting benchmark.

## Visual convention: now checked in full text

Figure 4, printed p. **05026021-5** (PDF page 5), shows 20 daily flow/pressure profiles over clock time with prominent curves labeled Average. The rendered page was visually inspected. This verifies the general **daily-overlay-plus-mean convention**, including the pressure panels resembling the example supplied in the project discussion. It does not prove the student's historical thought process, that the plot design is unique, or that the BattLeDIM figure is an identical adaptation.

The supplied rationale attributes inspiration to earlier field-data experience and this paper. Keep that as an attributed account pending personal review. The earlier repository attribution correctly recorded an illustrative plot supplied during discussion; the new account provides additional proposed context and does not retroactively prove who designed or implemented the current code. Current BattLeDIM data curves and correlations are recomputed from the released dataset, not copied from the ASCE field data.

## Transfer and boundaries

| Transferable idea | Boundary for this project |
|---|---|
| Inspect daily pressure/flow patterns and departures | An EDA visualization is not a fitted forecast or evidence of causality. |
| Interpret remote monitoring points through elevation, network scope and losses | n1 is a relatively high, remote **measured** C target; formal critical-node status is unproved. |
| Treat operating conditions and demand as relevant context | Future demand, outflow and valve states are not known at forecast origin. |
| Distinguish identification data from evaluation data | Use chronological preparation and disclose prior inspection; do not claim a blind 2019 test. |

The prior article concerns a PRV-controlled real sector; this proposal concerns a simulated tank-fed area and supervised pressure prediction at +15 minutes. No field dataset, controller code, fitted parameter or reported score is transferred into the BattLeDIM experiments. A self-citation explains background and visual inspiration; it does not establish novelty. The source PDF is copyright ASCE and is **not newly copied** into this public repository; the DOI and locators provide the reference.

A formal review/sign-off has not\\nbeen supplied; the proposed first-person replacement paragraph is not publicly republished.\\n\\n\\r\\n# Anticipated research contribution\\n\\nPost-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. **AI-assisted draft pending student review.** This statement proposes future work; it does not assert independent student implementation or already-demonstrated novelty.\\n\\nThe proposed contribution is a controlled assessment of **whether hydraulically selected measurements add useful information for forecasting recorded n1 pressure 15 minutes ahead, beyond n1 history and calendar information**. The project would compare increasingly rich input sets using the same regression methods, target timestamps, chronological periods and error measures. It would report both improvements and failures to improve, rather than claim a new algorithm.\\n\\nThe V3 account also reports the student's relevance screening and closer examination of\\nsix selected studies. That human interpretive role is distinct from AI-assisted discovery,\\nautomated counts and programming. The [review-method account](../literature/REVIEW_METHOD_AND_STUDENT_ROLE.md)\\ncredits it as student-reported; the [six-study matrix](../literature/SIX_STUDY_CRITICAL_MATRIX.md)\\noffers a source-checked draft synthesis for confirmation. Neither records independent\\nexecution of the authors' models or proves personal understanding through a CSV entry.\\n\\nThe supplied engineering rationale favors a small tank-fed area with three monitored pressure nodes, measured tank level, pump inflow and a local demand measurement. [Static verification](SECTOR_AND_NODE_SELECTION_RATIONALE.md) supports that scope and n1's relative elevation/remoteness **among monitored nodes**. It does not establish a uniquely critical node or guaranteed feature value. The supplied prior-research context is documented in [method transfer](PRIOR_RESEARCH_AND_METHOD_TRANSFER.md); it is background, not evidence of current forecast performance.\\n\\n## What the experiments could add\\n\\n1. **A reproducible preparation protocol:** six signal series, past-only lags and known calendar values, explicit +15-minute labels and common eligible rows. Input availability is a timestamp-based assumption; real telemetry delays are not documented.\\n2. **An incremental-value result:** distinguish the information in peer pressures, storage/pump signals and local AMR. The central question is not which variable has the highest marginal correlation, but which addition reduces future error after n1 history is already available.\\n3. **An honest account of effect size:** compare MAE/RMSE in metres, centimetres and relative changes against the same-period persistence baseline. If gains are small, unstable or absent, report that outcome and the limits of the hydraulic selection argument.\\n\\n[Ahmed et al.](AHMED_2025_COMPARISON.md) already combine the five main C measurements for reconstruction-based leak detection. Our proposed future-pressure target and added AMR comparison are **extensions in task and evaluation**, not proof of worldwide first use. The historical literature inventory is incomplete and cannot establish priority.\\n\\n## What could contradict the rationale\\n\\nPersistence already has small descriptive error. Peer pressure signals may be redundant; T1 may describe state already visible in n1; pump flow may be useful only near operating changes or may add nothing; AMR_n1 may add noise. A high-elevation target may not be the most difficult or operationally important one. These are counterarguments the [matched experiments](EXPERIMENTAL_CONTRIBUTION_PLAN.md) must allow to win.\\n\\nExpected benefit is therefore conditional: a reproducible empirical answer about information value on this simulated network. No causal mechanism, field deployment benefit, error reduction, novelty-by-feature-combination or independent human authorship is claimed. Approval and personal reasoning must be supplied through the [student review log](STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md).\\n\\r\\n# Prior field research and what can transfer\\n\\nPost-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. NOTEBOOK MARKDOWN
# Milestone 2: reproducible descriptive evidence

This notebook accompanies the public DS675 repository. It was assembled and executed with OpenAI Codex assistance. It does not claim independent student implementation or human review.

We reproduce checks, Figures 2-4, persistence errors, correlations and nominal pipe distances. No learned model is trained, and no 2019 prediction score is computed. See `docs/ATTRIBUTION.md` for status and authorship.
## 1. Identify and verify the original inputs

The source is Vrachimis et al., Zenodo record **4017659**, CC-BY-4.0. The manifest fixes the original file URLs and MD5 values. Raw measurements are simulated observations, not field measurements. Downloaded files are ignored by Git rather than republished as large duplicates.
## 2. Check tables, then select the exploratory period

2018 and 2019 are checked for structure, missing values and timestamp agreement. Only **January-September 2018** is used for the EDA. The selected six columns are pressures n1/n4/n31, T1 level, PUMP_1 flow and nodal demand AMR_n1. Reading 2019 for structure is disclosed; it is not a 2019 forecast evaluation.
## 3. Construct +15-minute labels and persistence

A five-minute sample interval means **three rows = 15 minutes**. The period is cropped before shifting the target, so the final three origins are excluded. The future pressure is a label for analysis; it is not a predictor. Persistence predicts that the future pressure equals the current n1 pressure. Its error is future n1 minus current n1.
## 4. Recreate Figures 2 and 3

Each thin line is one of 273 complete days. The red curve is the mean at each clock-time slot. Correlations describe association, not causation or incremental forecasting value. The gold divider separates the future label from origin inputs. Portable fonts can change the pixels without changing the underlying values.
## 5. Check nominal hydraulic scope and pipe distances

Dijkstra computes the shortest sum of nominal, initially open pipe lengths from T1. Pumps and PRVs define sector boundaries in this graph. These lengths are **not** pressure propagation delays, travel times or hydraulic sensitivities. The report printed nearest-metre distances; the full values and pipe IDs are retained below. Figure 1 is an interpretive schematic; its original source and image are archived separately.
## 6. Recount the historical literature inventory and recreate Figure 4

The historical inventory contains **47 source records grouped into 35 related-work groups**. Papers, versions and repositories can belong to one group. Category assignments are AI-assisted scholarly judgments recorded in CSVs. Recounting them does not establish an exhaustive search or independent human validation. Ahmed et al. (2025), identified after feedback, is listed separately and is not silently added to these counts.
## 7. What this demonstrates, and what remains proposed

The calculations can be executed from the original public data. This supports numerical traceability, not independent student authorship or proof of a novel forecasting method.

Gradient Boosting, Ridge, Random Forest, a final 2019 assessment and A/B/C feature-value comparisons remain proposed. Prior authors' model scores are literature-reported; their training was not reproduced.

For input/code/output hashes and checks against historical values, run `python reproduce.py --download` and inspect `results/reproduced/verification.json` and `run_manifest.json`. Record your own execution, understanding and unresolved questions honestly.

provenance\notebook_execution.json:3:  "python_version": "3.14.4 (tags/v3.14.4:23116f9, Apr  7 2026, 14:10:54) [MSC v.1944 64 bit (AMD64)]",

"""Check documentation links, archived hashes and completed audit evidence; no training."""
import argparse
import csv
import hashlib
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
import sys
from urllib.parse import unquote
import urllib.request

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
sys.path.insert(0, str(ROOT))
from repro.data import digest


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
    parser.add_argument('--reproduction-dir', type=Path,
                        help='Optional fresh reproduction to compare with committed numeric outputs.')
    parser.add_argument('--verify-literature-code', action='store_true',
                        help='Download nine pinned source files and check hashes without executing them.')
    args = parser.parse_args()
    broken, links = [], 0
    for path in [ROOT / 'README.md', *sorted((ROOT / 'docs').glob('*.md')),
                 *sorted((ROOT / 'literature').glob('*.md'))]:
        for url in re.findall(r'\[[^\]]*\]\(([^\s]+?)\)', path.read_text(encoding='utf-8')):
            if url.startswith(('https://', 'http://', '#', 'mailto:')):
                continue
            links += 1
            target = path.parent / unquote(url.split('#')[0].strip('<>'))
            if not target.exists():
                broken.append(f'{path.relative_to(ROOT)}: {url}')
    if broken:
        raise AssertionError(broken)
    historical = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/historical_artifacts.json').read_text())
    for item in historical['artifacts']:
        assert digest(ROOT / item['file']) == item['sha256'], item['file']
    instructions = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/engineering_prompt/v2_1_verified_instructions.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    assert digest(ROOT / 'provenance/engineering_prompt/v2_original_as_received.json') == instructions['audit_corrections']['source_sha256']
    geometry_dir = ROOT / 'results/engineering'
    manifest = json.loads((geometry_dir / 'audit_manifest.json').read_text())
    for group in ['code_sha256', 'protocol_sha256']:
        for name, expected in manifest[group].items():
            assert digest(ROOT / name) == expected, name
    for name, expected in manifest['output_sha256'].items():
        assert digest(geometry_dir / name) == expected, name
    archived_run = json.loads((ROOT / 'results/reproduced/run_manifest.json').read_text())
    for name, expected in archived_run['code_sha256'].items():
        assert digest(ROOT / name) == expected, name
    available, omitted = [], []
    for name, expected in archived_run['output_sha256'].items():
        path = ROOT / 'results/reproduced' / name
        if path.exists():
            assert digest(path) == expected, name
            available.append(name)
        else:
            # Full derived tables are intentionally Git-ignored and may be absent in a clone.
            assert name in {'selected_inputs_full.csv.gz', 'forecast_pairs_full.csv.gz'}, name
            omitted.append(name)
    notebook = json.loads((ROOT / 'notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    cells = [c for c in notebook['cells'] if c['cell_type'] == 'code']
    assert all(c.get('execution_count') is not None for c in cells)
    assert not any(o['output_type'] == 'error' for c in cells for o in c.get('outputs', []))
    compared = []
    if args.reproduction_dir:
        for name in ['eda_statistics.json', 'correlations.csv', 'topology_audit.json',
                     'screening_summary.json', 'table_checks.json', 'verification.json']:
            assert digest(args.reproduction_dir / name) == digest(ROOT / 'results/reproduced' / name), name
            compared.append(name)
    literature = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/literature_v3_source_verification.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    with (ROOT / 'literature/group_assignments.csv').open(encoding='utf-8-sig', newline='') as handle:
        groups = list(csv.DictReader(handle))
    selected = {r['group'] for r in groups if r['selected'] == 'True'}
    assert selected == set(literature['historical_selected_group_ids']) and len(selected) == 6
    assert len(groups) == literature['counts']['historical_groups'] == 35
    with (ROOT / 'literature/inventory_sources.csv').open(encoding='utf-8-sig', newline='') as handle:
        assert sum(1 for _ in csv.DictReader(handle)) == literature['counts']['historical_records'] == 47
    with (ROOT / 'literature/papers_catalog.csv').open(encoding='utf-8-sig', newline='') as handle:
        catalog = {row['document']: row for row in csv.DictReader(handle)}
    assert {paper['group'] for paper in literature['papers']} == selected
    included_papers, paper_references = 0, 0
    for paper in literature['papers']:
        if paper.get('sha256'):
            paper_references += 1
            assert paper['sha256'] == catalog[paper['document']]['sha256'], paper['document']
            if paper['public_pdf']:
                assert digest(ROOT / 'literature' / paper['public_pdf']) == paper['sha256']
                included_papers += 1
    assert len(literature['code_sources']) == 9
    source_downloads = 0
    for source in literature['code_sources']:
        assert source['commit'] in source['url'] and len(source['sha256']) == 64
        if args.verify_literature_code:
            with urllib.request.urlopen(source['url'], timeout=30) as response:
                assert hashlib.sha256(response.read()).hexdigest() == source['sha256'], source['id']
            source_downloads += 1
    account = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/literature_provenance_source.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    revised = json.loads((ROOT / 'provenance/literature_prompt/v3_1_verified_instructions.json').read_text(encoding='utf-8'))
    assert account['source_sha256'] == revised['audit_corrections']['source_sha256']
    print(json.dumps({'status': 'PASS', 'local_markdown_links_checked': links,
                      'broken_links': broken, 'historical_artifacts_hash_checked': len(historical['artifacts']),
                      'archived_output_hashes_checked': len(available), 'ignored_outputs_absent': omitted,
                      'engineering_manifest_matches': True, 'prompt_archive_hash_matches': True,
                      'notebook_code_cells_inspected': len(cells), 'notebook_cell_errors': 0,
                      'notebook_reexecuted_by_this_check': False,
                      'fresh_numeric_outputs_byte_identical': compared,
                      'literature_selected_groups_match': sorted(selected),
                      'literature_catalog_hash_references_checked': paper_references,
                      'included_primary_PDF_bytes_hash_checked': included_papers,
                      'code_source_locator_records_checked': 9,
                      'pinned_source_downloads_hash_verified': source_downloads,
                      'external_code_reexecuted_by_this_check': False,
                      'student_personal_reading_certified_by_this_check': False,
                      'models_trained': False}, indent=2))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()

# Literature review method and the student's reported role

Post-feedback clarification, 2026-10-28. The personal account was supplied in the user's V3 prompt. It is recorded here as **student-reported**, separately from the new Codex-assisted source checks. Final personal confirmation remains pending; no reading dates, signature or independent experiment execution are invented.

## A focused relevance review

The purpose was to identify preceding approaches relevant to predicting a measured node's pressure 15 minutes ahead, rather than conduct a general systematic review. The historical report, **p. 2, section 2**, explicitly states:

> This inventory is not exhaustive.

This exact sentence was checked in the five-page report identified by [its archived SHA-256](../provenance/historical_artifacts.json). The V3 prompt joins the report's opening search sentence and this closing qualification into a shortened account; they are separated by inventory/category details in the actual report and should not be presented as one continuous quotation.

| Phase | Actor and account | What this supports | What it does not establish |
|---|---|---|---|
| Discovery | AI-assisted exploratory searching followed BattLeDIM/L-Town links, citations and code pointers. | A candidate collection for a course review. | An exhaustive independent student database search or a complete retained search log. |
| Relevance screening | The student reports starting with abstracts and checking additional sections when nodal pressure prediction/estimation appeared relevant; works on other tasks were deprioritized. | An attributed account of human relevance judgments. | Equal-depth full-text reading of all 35 groups or personal validation of every classification. |
| Closer examination | The student reports examining ChebNet, SAM-kNN, AGN, LILA, GATRes and EILF more closely, emphasizing preparation, methods, evaluation and relevance to the proposed task. | An attributed account of focused reading and synthesis. | Execution of six codebases, reproduction of their scores or a full repository audit. |

The reported student contribution is the task-driven screening and interpretation: distinguishing future prediction from current reconstruction and leak diagnosis, recognizing changes in data provenance, and considering what can transfer to n1(+15). This is a meaningful intellectual role distinct from source discovery and from programming. Specific personal judgments should be confirmed or revised by the student rather than inferred from the inventory.

## Three counts, three meanings

- **47 source records** include papers, versions and repositories.
- **35 groups of related works** combine records describing a related approach; they are not 35 equally scrutinized papers.
- **Six selected groups** anchor the report's detailed discussion. EILF here is Wang et al.'s 2022 estimation, identification and localization framework, represented by the historical `tsinghua` group.

[The CSVs and recount procedure](SEARCH_AND_SCREENING.md) make the denominator and categories inspectable. They do not certify personal reading, scientific correctness of every grouping, exhaustive coverage or worldwide novelty. No PRISMA protocol or systematic-review status is claimed. A focused exploratory/narrative review fits the milestone's request to discuss relevant developers' solutions; it remains insufficient for a universal priority claim.

## Access and chronological boundaries

Access is not uniform. Some primary PDFs are openly licensed and included; others were read locally and remain linked because redistribution permission was not established. AGN's new check used publisher text returned through search indexing: direct retrieval returned HTTP 403 and some formulas/figures were absent. The new [six-study matrix](SIX_STUDY_CRITICAL_MATRIX.md) and [source ledger](../provenance/literature_v3_source_verification.json) identify what was actually checked now. This later audit does not prove which exact sections the student read earlier.

Ahmed et al. (2025) was identified through professor feedback and is a **post-feedback supplement**, not one of the historical six or a silent addition to the 35-group chart. [Its separate comparison](../docs/AHMED_2025_COMPARISON.md) explains the shared five C signals and different reconstruction/detection task.

The original V3 file remains local and unchanged; [its hash and receipt record](../provenance/literature_provenance_source.json) preserve the supplied account. The proposed first-person replacement paragraph is **not republished here** before approval. Use the [student review log](../docs/STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md) to confirm the screening sequence, actual reading and personal interpretation before submission.

"""Recount the published historical inventory; do not claim to repeat a web search."""
import csv
from .data import ROOT

CATEGORIES = [('leak', 'Leak detection / localization'), ('state', 'Current-state estimation'),
              ('forecast', 'Pressure/head forecasting'), ('anomaly', 'Anomalies / sensor faults'),
              ('sensors', 'Sensor placement')]


def recount():
    with (ROOT / 'literature/inventory_sources.csv').open(encoding='utf-8-sig', newline='') as handle:
        sources = list(csv.DictReader(handle))
    with (ROOT / 'literature/group_assignments.csv').open(encoding='utf-8-sig', newline='') as handle:
        groups = list(csv.DictReader(handle))
    mapping = {group['group']: group for group in groups}
    assert len(mapping) == len(groups), 'Duplicate group identifier'
    assert {row['group'] for row in sources} == set(mapping), 'Inventory/group mismatch'
    for row in sources:
        assert row['category'] == mapping[row['group']]['category']
        assert row['selected'].lower() == mapping[row['group']]['selected'].lower()
    categories = []
    for key, label in CATEGORIES:
        selected = [g for g in groups if g['category'] == key]
        categories.append({'category': key, 'label': label, 'groups': len(selected),
                           'percent': 100*len(selected)/len(groups),
                           'selected': sum(g['selected'].lower() == 'true' for g in selected),
                           'group_ids': [g['group'] for g in selected]})
    assert sum(row['groups'] for row in categories) == len(groups)
    return {
        'source_records': len(sources), 'groups': len(groups),
        'selected_groups': sum(g['selected'].lower() == 'true' for g in groups),
        'categories': categories,
        'status': 'Reproduction of the historical v0.30 inventory, not an exhaustive systematic review.',
        'attribution': 'AI-assisted source collection/grouping; counts computed from the supplied CSV files.',
        'Ahmed_2025_in_historical_inventory': False,
        'limitation': 'Code verifies grouping/count consistency, not the completeness of search or correctness of every scholarly classification.',
    }


The notebook `notebooks/01_milestone2_evidence.ipynb` walks through these operations.
Install `requirements-notebook.txt`, open it in an editor with Jupyter support, and select
the Python interpreter from `.venv`. For an automated execution with that environment:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/execute_notebook.py
```

## 4. Record an actual human review

Automatic output demonstrates computational execution, not individual understanding.
You can record the date of your own run, the commit used, outputs compared, concepts
understood and unresolved questions. No signature or human-review record is invented.

## What has NOT been done here

Gradient Boosting, Ridge and Random Forest have not been trained; their performance on
2019 has not been measured; and auxiliary-signal value has not been established. Published
model training was not reproduced either. Retain these boundaries when using this evidence
in a response to the professor or in the next report.

- **RQ3, optional:** Are improvements concentrated near predefined origin-time operating changes? Treat small-sample results cautiously.

# From engineering reasoning to testable ML hypotheses

Post-feedback addition, 2026-10-28. AI-assisted draft pending student review. The target is recorded **n1(t+15 min)**. Every predictor, transformation and inferred state must use only information available at origin t. Real measurement latency is an assumption to disclose, not a verified field property.

For a tank with water depth h and effective horizontal area A(h), continuity gives
`A(h) dh/dt = Q_in - Q_out`, with consistent flow/time units. Pressure head at a connected junction also depends on elevation, losses and network state. These physical relations motivate features; they are not a fitted forecast model. T1 depth is not itself absolute water-surface elevation. PUMP_1 measures inflow, while unmeasured aggregate outflow remains essential. AMR_n1 measures nodal consumption in L/h, not total C outflow.

The [static audit](../results/engineering/model_geometry_verification.json) also shows level-based pump controls. Pump flow can respond to storage state as well as influence it. Consequently, a useful pump feature would not establish a one-way causal effect. Nominal/generator tank diameters differ; no geometry-based future level estimate is introduced here.
Reproduce the optional generator check after the ordinary data download:

```powershell
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe scripts/audit_engineering_rationale.py --download-generator
```

This downloads an additional 177 MB into ignored `data/raw/`. A verified cache can instead be passed with `--generator path/to/L-TOWN_Real.inp`. Only static sections are retained; no forecast is evaluated. [Student questions](STUDENT_REVIEW_LOG.md) distinguish agreement with this rationale from actual personal execution.
- **[E]** Wang et al. (2022), [journal article](https://doi.org/10.1061/%28ASCE%29WR.1943-5452.0001558), PDF pp. 2-3, 6-7 and 9-11. Code is available on author request according to p. 11; no executable implementation was inspected. The [conference report](papers/P08a_Tsinghua-Team.pdf) and journal are related but differ: the conference's VAR mention does not establish a journal future-pressure horizon. EILF here is **estimation, identification and localization**, not the separate 2026 interpretable ensemble paper.

TP/FP/FN mean correctly accepted matches, false alarms/incorrect matches and missed events under each paper's protocol. Recall/TPR, precision and F1 describe detection quality; EUR is a competition score. They are not interchangeable with pressure MAE in metres, and alarm definitions/denominators differ between papers. Even current-pressure MAE cannot be ranked against a future n1 forecast without matching data, timestamps and targets.

## Draft synthesis and post-feedback supplement

The six cases motivate three distinct reference roles: ChebNet/SAM/AGN contain future-state prediction; LILA/GATRes clarify current-state reconstruction; EILF supports hydraulic preparation. A historical **primary-task category** can differ from this intermediate-model role. The proposed contribution should therefore test extra-signal value under common forecasting conditions rather than claim pressure prediction or tank-balance preparation is new. This synthesis is an AI-assisted draft for the student's confirmation/revision, not a reconstructed historical personal note.

**Ahmed et al. (2025), added after feedback:** the five Area C signals are already combined for daily-window reconstruction/leak detection. The separate [page-specific comparison](../docs/AHMED_2025_COMPARISON.md) gives preparation and reported detection results. # DS675 Mini-Project: BattLeDIM / L-Town

**Research lead:** Andres Arango Martinez. **Task:** predict recorded pressure at **n1, 15 minutes ahead**, using information available at the forecast origin.

The author proposed the target, local signals and comparisons and made the analyses and research decisions. His control-engineering motivation is to investigate advance pressure information that could later support delay-aware control and smoother tank-level regulation. AI supports writing, organization and code under his instructions. See [contribution and control motivation](docs/RESEARCH_CONTRIBUTION.md).

Code checks eight SCADA tables and reproduces Figures 2-4, correlations, persistence errors and nominal pipe paths. |

# Literature and paper copies

This release catalogs **48 documents**: **32 unmodified PDFs included**, with original links for the others. This is a document-access catalog, not the 35-group analytical denominator.

Each included document has an explicit license in its original PDF or official source record. All PDF bytes are unchanged; SHA-256, source URL and license evidence are recorded in [papers_catalog.csv](papers_catalog.csv). Full titles and author bylines remain in the PDFs; historical filenames in this index are labels, not replacement bibliographic citations.

**Third-party rights:** each PDF retains its original license and copyright. CC-BY attribution is retained in the PDF and this index. The IFAC ChebNet article is CC-BY-NC-ND: this is an unmodified copy for academic, noncommercial study. No translation, redlining or modification of those papers is supplied. No repository-wide license overrides paper licenses.

ASCE journal copies and other documents without established redistribution permission are linked, not republished. An openly licensed conference report is not silently represented as the later journal article. Publisher results cited from a journal must still be checked in that journal version. Institutional access does not, by itself, authorize a public GitHub copy.

Ahmed et al. (2025) is a **post-feedback addition**, outside the frozen historical inventory. Its official Sheffield metadata and file checksum are supplied separately.

For the author-directed discovery, relevance screening and focused reading process, see [review method and student role](REVIEW_METHOD_AND_STUDENT_ROLE.md). The
[six-study critical matrix](SIX_STUDY_CRITICAL_MATRIX.md) links source-checked methods,
reported results and limits of comparison with n1(+15). The author interprets the reviewed studies; source checking is distinct from reproducing their experiments.

# How to verify the Milestone 2 work

This repository follows the chain **data -> code -> calculation -> figure -> claim**.
The research design, analyses and interpretation are by Andres Arango Martinez. Code was
generated and refined with AI assistance under his instructions.

## 1. Run the analysis

Clone the repository with GitHub Desktop and open its folder. With Python 3.14 installed:

```powershell
python -m venv .venv
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pip install -r requirements.txt
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe reproduce.py --download
.\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m unittest discover -s tests -v
```

The command downloads the required files from the original Zenodo record 4017659.
It checks MD5 values and also records SHA-256 hashes. Institutional credentials are not
needed. Large raw inputs remain in the Git-ignored `data/raw/` folder; small outputs and
figures are published for convenient inspection.

````
